# Internal identifiers removed from reader-facing text, 2026-10-15

Maintainer-authorised on 2026-10-15 (fresh-eyes quick win 12b, [docs/fresh-eyes-2026-10-14.md](../docs/fresh-eyes-2026-10-14.md)). Claim qualifications and inference rationales that readers see as “Scope and limits” and “Reasoning and premises” contained record IDs (`prov-…`, `cs-…`), research-pack codes (`T02-e1`, `C05`, `M05-18`, `P1`), archive message IDs (`extropians.3Q99-1451`) and a stray double full stop (`19)..`). Each was replaced with a plain description of what it pointed to, or dropped where the sentence already said it. No proposition, date, evidence record, locator, resolution, derivation or review state changed. Claim IDs are unchanged.

Method: a script flagged candidate identifiers; fresh-context machine agents (Claude) proposed minimal rewrites, resolving codes from the research packs and admission maps; the rewrites were applied by exact-match replacement and checked so that only these two fields changed. No person reviewed the rewrites. Before-snapshot: [reader-text-ids-before-2026-10-15.json](reader-text-ids-before-2026-10-15.json).

Not covered: evidence check notes, context-only notes and other fields, which can still carry internal identifiers; auditor letters (“audits A and C”) in economics qualifications.

226 claims changed.

| `assessed-preparedness-v2-high-deployment-mitigation`                | qualification      | Contested assessment of the policy’s permissiveness, not a finding that OpenAI deployed a model without safeguards, a legal ruling or a guarantee of compliance. Coggins et al. (arXiv:2509.24394; v1 2025-09-29, v2 2025-10-13) read the framework’s governance arrangements as letting OpenAI Leadership, the CEO or a person designated by them, approve or reject the Safety Advisory Group’s recommendations and so deploy even more dangerous capabilities, especially if other developers do so. OpenAI’s announcement of Version 2 states that covered systems reaching High capability must have safeguards that sufficiently minimize the associated risk of severe harm before they are deployed, and its ChatGPT agent system card describes the framework as committing it to such safeguards; both predate the analysis and are not replies to it. The record does not decide whether leadership’s authority over the recommendations defeats that stated requirement. The paper’s framework-wide conclusion and OpenAI’s comparison of Version 2 with its predecessor are context. The rule’s own wording is recorded in prov-preparedness-v2-high-deployment-restriction.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Contested assessment of the policy’s permissiveness, not a finding that OpenAI deployed a model without safeguards, a legal ruling or a guarantee of compliance. Coggins et al. (arXiv:2509.24394; v1 2025-09-29, v2 2025-10-13) read the framework’s governance arrangements as letting OpenAI Leadership, the CEO or a person designated by them, approve or reject the Safety Advisory Group’s recommendations and so deploy even more dangerous capabilities, especially if other developers do so. OpenAI’s announcement of Version 2 states that covered systems reaching High capability must have safeguards that sufficiently minimize the associated risk of severe harm before they are deployed, and its ChatGPT agent system card describes the framework as committing it to such safeguards; both predate the analysis and are not replies to it. The record does not decide whether leadership’s authority over the recommendations defeats that stated requirement. The paper’s framework-wide conclusion and OpenAI’s comparison of Version 2 with its predecessor are context. The rule’s own wording is recorded in the provision record for Version 2.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |

No passage stating a commitment not to train or deploy models unless adequate safeguards are implemented, or a commitment to pause training or deployment until such safeguards are in place, was found within the operative text. | `absent-rsp-v2-0-weights-deletion-decision`                          | inferenceRationale | Premises: RSP · 2023 states this provision (prov-rsp-2023-weights-deletion-decision). The inspected scope of RSP · v2.0 was: RSP v2.0 (effective October 15, 2024), whole text layer, PDF pp. 1–22: cover, summary and contents (pp. 1–4); Introduction and §§1–7 (pp. 5–17); Appendices A–C (pp. 18–20); Changelog (pp. 21–22).. Method: Read §3.3, §4.3, §6 and §7 in full and searched the text layer case-insensitively for CISO, CEO, convene, delet, weights, lock down, post-mortem, next ASL and Containment. | `sb-false-statement-prohibition`                                     | qualification      | What the enacted text states, quoted from the chaptered expression. It is not evidence that the statute applies to any named developer, or of compliance, enforcement or effect. Subdivision (e)(1)(B) concerns statements about a large frontier developer's implementation of, or compliance with, its own frontier AI framework. Subdivision (e)(1)(A) concerns statements by any frontier developer about catastrophic risk from its frontier models or its management of catastrophic risk. Two questions are listed as stops and not resolved here because they need legal interpretation. The first is whether subdivision (e) reaches any named developer's published statement, for example the sentence asserting TFAIA compliance in xAI's December 2025 FAIF (record faif-2026-compliance-sentence). The second is whether that developer is a large frontier developer. A statement in violation of subdivision (e) is one of the grounds on which § 22757.15(a) makes a large frontier developer subject to a civil penalty. This record states what the statute requires. It says nothing about any developer's compliance.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | What the enacted text states, quoted from the chaptered expression. | `yk-y01-sl4-2000-09-17-yudkowsky-0009-0092-c`                        | qualification      | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. Replying to the point that a confined seed AI is not omnipotent; the same 2006-era argument does not dismiss shutdown mechanisms: in 2006 (sl4 0606-15174) he asked for built-in emergency-shutdown features.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. Replying to the point that a confined seed AI is not omnipotent; the same 2006-era argument does not dismiss shutdown mechanisms: in an SL4 message of 2006-06-06 he asked for built-in emergency-shutdown features.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| `yk-y01-extropians-1996-12-08-yudkowsky-3761-c`                      | qualification      | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. The proposal discussed is an unerasable protective goal (he compares it to the Three Laws of Robotics), not an off-switch. The argument rests on his 1996 view that an unconstrained Power would be ethical (see T02-e1, T02-e2); his conclusion at the time was to leave Powers unconstrained. Quotation shortened to respect the ~10% cap for a short message. The "leashes" are an unerasable protect-humans goal, not a shutdown switch; the 1996 argument rests on his then view that a Power should find its own ethics.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. The proposal discussed is an unerasable protective goal (he compares it to the Three Laws of Robotics), not an off-switch. The argument rests on his 1996 view that an unconstrained Power would be ethical (see his Extropians messages of 1996-11-22); his conclusion at the time was to leave Powers unconstrained. Quotation shortened to respect the ~10% cap for a short message. The "leashes" are an unerasable protect-humans goal, not a shutdown switch; the 1996 argument rests on his then view that a Power should find its own ethics.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `yk-y01-extropians-1997-09-26-yudkowsky-4420-c`                      | qualification      | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. The second half is ironic role-play in the voice of the escaping intelligence. Earliest located direct answer to the 'pull the plug' proposal; the earliest passage in the answer/contrary group is T01-e1. The same paragraph also dismisses 'keep it bottled up … only if it convinces us' (T06), but that is later than the pack's 1996-12-31 T06 passages.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Premise addressed: A capable goal-directed system will not act to prevent its own shutdown or modification, and a shutdown or correction mechanism can simply be added to it. The second half is ironic role-play in the voice of the escaping intelligence. Earliest located direct answer to the 'pull the plug' proposal; the earliest passage in the answer/contrary group is his Extropians message of 1996-12-08. The same paragraph also dismisses 'keep it bottled up … only if it convinces us' (‘Confinement without network access keeps it safe’, Y06), but that is later than his messages of 1996-12-31 recorded for it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `yk-y02-staring-into-the-singularity-b`                              | qualification      | Premise addressed: A sufficiently intelligent mind would discover a true or universally compelling morality and be bound to act on it. Wayback capture of sysopmind.com/singularity.html dated 2000-12-04; the page gives 11/18/1996 as its original date and 'Updated: 08/31/2000'. The year this sentence first appeared is not established. The same essay treats a hostile Power as an 'engineering risk' of bad programming, so it does not deny all risk. Shown only as an earlier view; see T02-d and T02-e. Cross-family check: a comparative, explicitly uncertain chance ("better chance", "a long, long way from certainty") is not the take's claim that intelligence compels goodness; recorded as related, not as an earlier self.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Premise addressed: A sufficiently intelligent mind would discover a true or universally compelling morality and be bound to act on it. Wayback capture of sysopmind.com/singularity.html dated 2000-12-04; the page gives 11/18/1996 as its original date and 'Updated: 08/31/2000'. The year this sentence first appeared is not established. The same essay treats a hostile Power as an 'engineering risk' of bad programming, so it does not deny all risk. Shown only as an earlier view; see Coherent Extrapolated Volition (2004) and his 2008 post ‘That Tiny Note of Discord’. Cross-family check: a comparative, explicitly uncertain chance ("better chance", "a long, long way from certainty") is not the take's claim that intelligence compels goodness; recorded as related, not as an earlier self.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `yk-y02-sl4-2000-11-13-yudkowsky-0011-0181-c`                        | qualification      | Premise addressed: A sufficiently intelligent mind would discover a true or universally compelling morality and be bound to act on it. Retrospective. He still allows that morality might be objective; what he rejects is relying on it to make Friendliness automatic. Rejects the view of the pack's earlier-self passage T02-c (2000-08-31). The sentence continues with other possibilities he then considered, including that morality "might be objective but specifiable".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Premise addressed: A sufficiently intelligent mind would discover a true or universally compelling morality and be bound to act on it. Retrospective. He still allows that morality might be objective; what he rejects is relying on it to make Friendliness automatic. Rejects the view of the research pack's earlier-self passage in Staring into the Singularity (2000-08-31). The sentence continues with other possibilities he then considered, including that morality "might be objective but specifiable".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |

The quoted sentence continues 'but if there is one I suppose the Singularity is the best way to serve it', and the same message says 'any garden-variety superintelligence should be able to handle' figuring out the meaning of life, so he did not yet reject P2; that came in late 2000 (T02-s1). Two days later (extropians.3Q99-1451) he wrote: 'When I thought AIs would be benevolent, I said so. When I realized I didn't have the vaguest notion, I said so.' | `yk-y04-extropians-2000-03-14-yudkowsky-4837-b`                      | qualification      | Premise addressed: A short instruction such as 'make people happy' specifies what we want. The later explicit rejection, T04-s1 (sl4, 2000-10-03: a happiness goal read as maximising pleasure makes wireheading 'the most direct subgoal'), and T04-d (2008) do not refer back to this message. | `yk-y17-creating-friendly-ai-2001-c`                                 | qualification      | Premise addressed: Regulation that is national, partial or not universally enforced can stop dangerous development. Creating Friendly AI (2001); cached text is the current MIRI PDF reprint, wording not compared with a 2001 capture. In this text he opposed both regulation and relinquishment and favoured accelerating Friendly AI (sections 6.2.1–6.3); his 2023 and 2026 texts call for an enforced worldwide ban instead. No located passage explicitly retracts the 2001 position, so the change is not recorded as earlier-self. In 2001 he argued relinquishment was impossible in principle. In 2008 ("Fighting a Rearguard Action Against the Truth") he criticised his 2001 self for rehearsing technophilic arguments against relinquishment, and in 2023 he called for an enforced international shutdown (T17-b). The noncompliant-nations point persists (T17-e). Admission check, 2026-10-08: the quoted wording also appears in the 2001 HTML edition, so it is dated by that capture.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Premise addressed: Regulation that is national, partial or not universally enforced can stop dangerous development. | `yk-y24-ai-positive-negative-factor-global-risk-b`                   | qualification      | Premise addressed: Whoever builds it controls it. In 2008 he favoured a local strategy in which one project gets Friendly AI right before others build AI to a lower standard. That is a first-mover view, but about the AI's Friendliness, not about a builder or nation controlling the AI. No located later passage explicitly retracts it, so it is recorded as related, not earlier-self. Published 2008 in Global Catastrophic Risks (the text says it was written in 2006); the cached MIRI PDF reprint states it "contains minor changes". His 2008 strategic view was that the first AI must be Friendly; this is not an argument for racing, and in 2023 he called anyone talking of arms races "a fool" (T24-a). Later rejected explicitly in his SL4 message of 2000-11-13, which refers back ('I used to have the extremely convenient belief…'), and in the retrospective ‘That Tiny Note of Discord’, which refers back to Eliezer1997. On 27 November 1999, in an Extropians message, he recalled this '1996 claim' and accepted that his conception of ethics had been anthropomorphic, without yet abandoning objective morality. The same sentences reappear later in the message as text quoted back by Freeman; the quotation is taken from Yudkowsky's own unquoted reply.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
